<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every smooth almost complex structure on a closed four-manifold which is tamed by a symplectic form is compatible with a symplectic form. This gives a positive solution to Donaldson's tamed-to-compatible conjecture. We also characterize the compatible cone by strict intersection positivity on the closure of the invariant part of the taming cone.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Suppose that a smooth almost complex structure $J$ on a closed connected smooth real four-manifold $X$ is tamed by a symplectic form. Then there is a smooth symplectic form compatible with $J$.
  - Explanation: A taming symplectic form guarantees existence of a compatible form for the given almost complex structure; no prescribed class is asserted.
- **C2** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-taming-cones)): Let $X,J$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and give $X$ the orientation induced by $J$. Let $\mathcal K_J^t,\mathcal K_J^c\subset H^2(X;\mathbb R)$ be the classes represented by taming and compatible symplectic forms, respectively. Let $H_J^-$ be the subspace represented by smooth closed real anti-invariant two-forms. Set $$\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\perp,\qquad \mathcal C=\mathcal K_J^t\cap\mathcal V,$$ where orthogonality is for the intersection pairing. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal K_J^t&=\mathcal C+H_J^-,\label{eq:tame-decomposition}\\ \mathcal K_J^c&= \{a\in\mathcal C:\ a\cdot y>0 \text{ for every }0\ne y\in\overline{\mathcal C}\}. \label{eq:compatible-duality} \end{aligned}$$ The closure is taken in $\mathcal V$, and the sum in <a href="#eq:tame-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tame-decomposition">[eq:tame-decomposition]</a> is a Minkowski sum.
  - Explanation: The taming cone splits along anti-invariant cohomology, and compatible classes satisfy strict intersection positivity against the nonzero closure of its invariant slice.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-taming-cones)): Under the same hypotheses, write $h_J^-=\dim H_J^-$ and let $b_2^+(X)$ be the positive index of the intersection form. If $h_J^-=b_2^+(X)-1$, then $$\mathcal K_J^t=\mathcal K_J^c+H_J^-.$$ In particular, if $b_2^+(X)=1$, then $\mathcal K_J^t=\mathcal K_J^c$: every taming class has a smooth compatible symplectic representative.
  - Explanation: At the stated cohomological dimension equality the taming cone is the compatible cone plus anti-invariant classes; with positive index one the cones coincide.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-lift)): There is a real order-zero pseudodifferential operator $$B:C^\infty(\operatorname{im}R)\longrightarrow\Omega^2(X)$$ such that $dB=0$ and $RB=\mathrm{id}$.
  - Explanation: The elliptic lift produces closed forms with the specified anti-invariant projection; positivity is a separate requirement.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-separation)): If $J$ admits no compatible symplectic form, there are currents $P,Q,T$ and a finite positive measure $\lambda$ on $\mathscr C$ such that $$\begin{aligned} P(\alpha)&=\int_{\mathscr C}\alpha_x(\ell)\,d\lambda(x,\ell), &\lambda(\mathscr C)&=1,\label{eq:positive-representation}\\ T&=P+Q, &RP&=0,\quad RQ=Q,\quad *Q=Q,\label{eq:decomposition}\\ T(\alpha)&=0&&\text{for every smooth closed two-form }\alpha. \label{eq:annihilation} \end{aligned}$$ The current $P$ annihilates every closed invariant two-form. The projection $\mu$ of $\lambda$ to $X$ is the *trace measure* of $P$: for every smooth function $b$, $P(bF)=\int_Xb\,d\mu$. Each of $P,Q,T$ belongs to $H^{-3}$.
  - Explanation: Failure of compatibility produces a positive current and an anti-invariant correction whose sum annihilates smooth closed forms.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-growth)): There is a constant $C$ such that $$\label{eq:growth} \mu(B_s(x))\le Cs^2$$ for all $x\in X$ and $s>0$.
  - Explanation: Corrected radial tests yield uniform quadratic growth of the separating trace measure.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-radial-correction)): Choose normal coordinates centered at $x$, on a uniformly small fixed ball, and put $t=d_g(x,y)$. Let a smooth radial cutoff be one on a smaller ball and vanish outside the coordinate ball. For $s>0$ small, let $u_s,h_s$ be this cutoff times $$\log\sqrt{s^2+t^2},\qquad \sqrt{s^2+t^2},$$ respectively, extended by zero. Near the center, $$\label{eq:correction-bounds} \|BRdd_J^cu_s\|\le \frac{C}{s+t}, \qquad \|BRdd_J^ch_s\|\le C(1+\|\log(s+t)\|).$$ Both expressions are uniformly bounded at any fixed positive distance from $x$.
  - Explanation: The closed-lift correction of the radial logarithm and square root has the stated local bounds.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-pairing)): If $T'$ is a closed current with $S_rT'\in L^2$, then $$\label{eq:zero-pairing} \left\langle S_rT,*S_rT'\right\rangle_{L^2}=0.$$
  - Explanation: Annihilation of closed forms forces the regularized pairing with a closed current to vanish.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel)): The continuous kernel $K_r(x,y)$ of $S_r^2$ can be written $$K_r(x,y)=p_r(x,y)\tau_{yx}+E_r(x,y),$$ where $p_r\ge0$ is supported in a fixed neighborhood of the diagonal. For every fixed positive integer $N$, $$\begin{aligned} 0\le p_r(x,y)&\le C_Nr^{-4}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N}, \label{eq:p-upper}\\ p_r(x,y)&\ge cr^{-4}\qquad(d_g(x,y)<r), \label{eq:p-lower}\\ \|E_r(x,y)\|&\le C_Nr^{-2}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N}. \label{eq:error-kernel} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The squared resolvent kernel has a positive parallel-transport term and a remainder with uniform scale-dependent bounds.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-pairing)): Uniformly for all $\lambda'\le\lambda$, $$\label{eq:smoothed-mass} \\|S_rP'\\|_2\le Cr^{-1},$$ and, writing $d=d_g(x,y)$, $$\begin{aligned} \left\langle S_rP,*S_rP'\right\rangle_{L^2} &\ge cr^{-4}\iint_{d<r} \|\ell-\tau_{yx}m\|^2\,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda'(y,m) \nonumber\\ &\quad-Cr^{-2}\iint(1+d/r)^{-6}\,d\mu(x)\,d\mu'(y). \label{eq:positive-pairing} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover $$\label{eq:shell} r^{-2}\int_X(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-6}\,d\mu(x)\le C.$$
  - Explanation: Quadratic growth controls smoothed mass and the negative pairing error while retaining a nonnegative angular term.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixing)): Let $\Pi$ be a fixed smooth orthogonal projection on $\Lambda^2T^*X$. If a distributional two-form $W$ satisfies $\Pi W=0$ and $S_rW\in L^2$, then $$\label{eq:mixing} \\|\Pi S_rW\\|_2\le Cr\\|S_rW\\|_2.$$ The constant may depend on $\Pi$, but is independent of $r$ and $W$.
  - Explanation: A fixed smooth projection has relative leakage tending to zero under resolvent smoothing when it annihilates the original distribution.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-energy)): The correction $Q$ belongs to $L^2$, and $$\label{eq:angular} \mathcal I_r:= \iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}\|\ell-\tau_{yx}m\|^2 \,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda(y,m)\le Cr^4.$$ For every positive current $P'$ represented by $\lambda'\le\lambda$, $$\label{eq:cross-vanish} \lim_{r\downarrow0}\left\langle S_rP',*S_rQ\right\rangle_{L^2}=0.$$
  - Explanation: The correction has square-integrable coefficients, nearby complex-line directions obey the angular bound, and the mixed pairing tends to zero.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-splitting)): Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold. Suppose that $$P(\alpha)=\int_{\mathscr C}\alpha_x(\ell)\,d\lambda(x,\ell)$$ for a finite positive measure $\lambda$, with projection $\mu$. Suppose also that $Q\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current. Assume the estimates $$\begin{aligned} \mu(B_r(x))&\le Cr^2,\label{eq:abstract-growth}\\ \iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}\|\ell-\tau_{yx}m\|^2 \,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda(y,m)&\le Cr^4 \label{eq:abstract-angular} \end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently small $r$, uniformly in $x$. Then the limit $$\theta(x)=\lim_{r\downarrow0}r^{-2}\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point. If $E=\{x:\theta(x)>0\}$, the current $P_E=\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.
  - Explanation: Closedness of the sum, square-integrability of the anti-invariant correction, and both geometric estimates imply existence of density and closedness of the positive-density restriction.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-density)): The two-density $\theta(p)$ exists and is finite at every $p\in X$.
  - Explanation: Under the splitting hypotheses the trace measure has finite density at every center.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:splitting">13</a>, $$\label{eq:transverse} \mathcal N_r:= \int_{\mathscr C}\int_{B_r(x)} \left\|\operatorname{pr}_{L_\ell^\perp}\frac{z_x(y)}r\right\|^2 \,d\mu(y)\,d\lambda(x,\ell)=o(r^2).$$
  - Explanation: Planar tangent measures give a vanishing normalized transverse moment without a quantitative convergence rate.
- **C16** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant-cohomology)): The classes represented by closed invariant forms are exactly $\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\perp$. The intersection form is positive definite on $H_J^-$, and $$H^2(X;\mathbb R)=\mathcal V\oplus H_J^-.$$ The operator $1-BR$ takes exact forms to exact invariant forms.
  - Explanation: Closed invariant representatives are exactly the intersection-orthogonal complement of anti-invariant cohomology; the lift also preserves exactness.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-class-separation)): Let $a\in\mathcal V$ have no compatible representative. There are currents $P,Q,T\in H^{-3}$, with $P$ nonzero and positive, such that $$\begin{gathered} P(F)=1,\qquad P\text{ annihilates exact invariant forms}, \label{eq:class-exact-tests}\\ T=P+Q\text{ is closed},\qquad RQ=Q=*Q, \label{eq:class-current}\\ \mathfrak c(T)\in\mathcal V,\qquad \mathfrak c(T)\cdot a\le0. \label{eq:class-separator-sign} \end{gathered}$$ Moreover, a class $b\in H^2(X;\mathbb R)$ is taming if and only if $$b\cdot\mathfrak c(N)>0 \quad\text{for every nonzero closed positive current }N.$$
  - Explanation: A class without a compatible representative has a separating closed current, while taming is characterized by positive pairing with every nonzero closed positive current.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cohomological-pairing)): For closed currents $D_1,D_2\in H^{-3}$, $$\label{eq:cohomological-pairing} \left\langle S_rD_1,*S_rD_2\right\rangle_{L^2} =\mathfrak c(D_1)\cdot\mathfrak c(D_2).$$
  - Explanation: For closed currents of the stated Sobolev regularity the resolvent pairing equals the intersection pairing of their dual classes.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-class-estimates)): Suppose that $P\ne0$ is positive, annihilates exact invariant forms, and $T=P+Q$ is closed, where $Q\in H^{-3}$ is anti-invariant. Write $\lambda$ for a positive measure representing $P$, and $\mu$ for its trace. Then $$\begin{gathered} \mu(B_s(x))\le Cs^2,\qquad Q\in L^2, \label{eq:class-growth}\\ \iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}\|\ell-\tau_{yx}m\|^2 \,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda(y,m)\le Cr^4. \label{eq:class-angular} \end{gathered}$$ Consequently the positive-density restriction $P_E$ is closed, where $E=\{x:\lim_{s\downarrow0}s^{-2}\mu(B_s(x))>0\}$.* *If $A$ is any positive current whose trace has quadratic growth, then $$\label{eq:class-mixed-limit} \lim_{r\downarrow0}\left\langle S_rA,*S_rQ\right\rangle_{L^2}=0.$$ Every closed positive current has quadratic trace growth.
  - Explanation: The current estimates extend from annihilation of closed invariant forms to annihilation of exact invariant forms, permitting prescribed-class separation.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-general-residual)): Let $A,P'$ be positive currents whose trace measures $\nu,\mu'$ have quadratic growth. Suppose $$\lim_{s\downarrow0}s^{-2}\mu'(B_s(x))=0 \quad\text{for }\mu'\text{-almost every }x.$$ Then $$\label{eq:general-residual} \liminf_{r\downarrow0}\left\langle S_rA,*S_rP'\right\rangle_{L^2}\ge0.$$
  - Explanation: A residual positive current with vanishing density almost everywhere has a nonnegative lower limiting pairing with a positive current of quadratic growth.
- **C21** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-ex-strict-cones)): On $X=\mathbb R^4/\mathbb Z^4$, use the Euclidean metric and standard orientation, and put $e^{ij}=dx_i\wedge dx_j$. Set $$U=e^{12}+e^{34},\qquad V_0=e^{13}-e^{24},\qquad W=e^{14}+e^{23},$$ $$f=\tfrac14\sin(2\pi x_1),\qquad k=\tfrac14\sin(2\pi x_2),\qquad r=(1+f^2+k^2)^{1/2}.$$ The form $F=(U+fV_0+kW)/r$ is self-dual and has squared norm two, so it is the fundamental form of a smooth orthogonal almost complex structure $J$ of this orientation. The form $$\eta=U+2f e^{13}+2k e^{23}$$ is closed. Its self-dual part is $rF$, so it is invariant, and its anti-self-dual part is $$f(e^{13}+e^{24})+k(e^{23}-e^{14}).$$ Its Hermitian eigenvalues are $r\pm\sqrt{f^2+k^2}>0$. Thus $\eta$ is compatible. The sine terms are exact, giving $[\eta]=[U]$. A closed anti-invariant form is self-dual and harmonic for the flat metric, hence is a constant combination $aU+bV_0+cW$. Orthogonality to $F$ gives $a+bf+ck=0$ everywhere. Varying $x_1,x_2$ yields $a=b=c=0$; thus $H_J^-=0$. The closed forms $U\pm V_0$ have invariant projections $(1\pm f)F/r$, so both tame $J$. Their classes pair to zero, since $U^2=V_0^2$. Neither class can have a compatible representative, which would wedge strictly positively with a tamer in the other class. Hence $$\mathcal K_J^c+H_J^-=\mathcal K_J^c \subsetneq\mathcal K_J^t.$$ In Theorem <a href="#thm:taming-cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:taming-cones">2</a>, the class $[U+V_0]$ fails the strict positivity test against $[U-V_0]\in\mathcal C$, and conversely.
  - Explanation: The flat torus example has a compatible form but taming classes without compatible representatives; it separates class realization from existence.

      "id": "C1",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that a smooth almost complex structure $J$ on a closed connected smooth real four-manifold $X$ is tamed by a symplectic form. Then there is a smooth symplectic form compatible with $J$.",
          "source_id": "PM-342.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-main"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-taming-cones",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,J$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, and give $X$ the orientation induced by $J$. Let $\\mathcal K_J^t,\\mathcal K_J^c\\subset H^2(X;\\mathbb R)$ be the classes represented by taming and compatible symplectic forms, respectively. Let $H_J^-$ be the subspace represented by smooth closed real anti-invariant two-forms. Set $$\\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\\perp,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C=\\mathcal K_J^t\\cap\\mathcal V,$$ where orthogonality is for the intersection pairing. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal K_J^t&=\\mathcal C+H_J^-,\\label{eq:tame-decomposition}\\\\\n \\mathcal K_J^c&=\n \\{a\\in\\mathcal C:\\ a\\cdot y>0\n   \\text{ for every }0\\ne y\\in\\overline{\\mathcal C}\\}.\n \\label{eq:compatible-duality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The closure is taken in $\\mathcal V$, and the sum in <a href=\"#eq:tame-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tame-decomposition\">[eq:tame-decomposition]</a> is a Minkowski sum.",
        "source_id": "PM-342.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-taming-cones",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,J$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, and give $X$ the orientation induced by $J$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-taming-cones",
        "exact_quote": "Under the same hypotheses, write $h_J^-=\\dim H_J^-$ and let $b_2^+(X)$ be the positive index of the intersection form.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-separation",
        "exact_quote": "If $J$ admits no compatible symplectic form, there are currents $P,Q,T$ and a finite positive measure $\\lambda$ on $\\mathscr C$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\nP(\\alpha)&=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell),\n&\\lambda(\\mathscr C)&=1,\\label{eq:positive-representation}\\\\\nT&=P+Q,\n&RP&=0,\\quad RQ=Q,\\quad *Q=Q,\\label{eq:decomposition}\\\\\nT(\\alpha)&=0&&\\text{for every smooth closed two-form }\\alpha.\n\\label{eq:annihilation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The current $P$ annihilates every closed invariant two-form. The projection $\\mu$ of $\\lambda$ to $X$ is the *trace measure* of $P$: for every smooth function $b$, $P(bF)=\\int_Xb\\,d\\mu$. Each of $P,Q,T$ belongs to $H^{-3}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-342.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-separation",
          "exact_quote": "If $J$ admits no compatible symplectic form, there are currents $P,Q,T$ and a finite positive measure $\\lambda$ on $\\mathscr C$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\nP(\\alpha)&=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell),\n&\\lambda(\\mathscr C)&=1,\\label{eq:positive-representation}\\\\\nT&=P+Q,\n&RP&=0,\\quad RQ=Q,\\quad *Q=Q,\\label{eq:decomposition}\\\\\nT(\\alpha)&=0&&\\text{for every smooth closed two-form }\\alpha.\n\\label{eq:annihilation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The current $P$ annihilates every closed invariant two-form. The projection $\\mu$ of $\\lambda$ to $X$ is the *trace measure* of $P$: for every smooth function $b$, $P(bF)=\\int_Xb\\,d\\mu$. Each of $P,Q,T$ belongs to $H^{-3}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-342.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $J$ admits no compatible symplectic form, there are currents $P,Q,T$ and a finite positive measure $\\lambda$ on $\\mathscr C$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\nP(\\alpha)&=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell),\n&\\lambda(\\mathscr C)&=1,\\label{eq:positive-representation}\\\\\nT&=P+Q,\n&RP&=0,\\quad RQ=Q,\\quad *Q=Q,\\label{eq:decomposition}\\\\\nT(\\alpha)&=0&&\\text{for every smooth closed two-form }\\alpha.\n\\label{eq:annihilation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The current $P$ annihilates every closed invariant two-form.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-radial-correction",
        "exact_quote": "Choose normal coordinates centered at $x$, on a uniformly small fixed ball, and put $t=d_g(x,y)$. Let a smooth radial cutoff be one on a smaller ball and vanish outside the coordinate ball. For $s>0$ small, let $u_s,h_s$ be this cutoff times $$\\log\\sqrt{s^2+t^2},\\qquad \\sqrt{s^2+t^2},$$ respectively, extended by zero. Near the center, $$\\label{eq:correction-bounds}\n|BRdd_J^cu_s|\\le \\frac{C}{s+t},\n\\qquad\n|BRdd_J^ch_s|\\le C(1+|\\log(s+t)|).$$ Both expressions are uniformly bounded at any fixed positive distance from $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-342.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "The continuous kernel $K_r(x,y)$ of $S_r^2$ can be written $$K_r(x,y)=p_r(x,y)\\tau_{yx}+E_r(x,y),$$ where $p_r\\ge0$ is supported in a fixed neighborhood of the diagonal. For every fixed positive integer $N$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n0\\le p_r(x,y)&\\le C_Nr^{-4}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N},\n\\label{eq:p-upper}\\\\\np_r(x,y)&\\ge cr^{-4}\\qquad(d_g(x,y)<r),\n\\label{eq:p-lower}\\\\\n|E_r(x,y)|&\\le C_Nr^{-2}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N}.\n\\label{eq:error-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-342.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "The continuous kernel $K_r(x,y)$ of $S_r^2$ can be written $$K_r(x,y)=p_r(x,y)\\tau_{yx}+E_r(x,y),$$ where $p_r\\ge0$ is supported in a fixed neighborhood of the diagonal. For every fixed positive integer $N$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n0\\le p_r(x,y)&\\le C_Nr^{-4}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N},\n\\label{eq:p-upper}\\\\\np_r(x,y)&\\ge cr^{-4}\\qquad(d_g(x,y)<r),\n\\label{eq:p-lower}\\\\\n|E_r(x,y)|&\\le C_Nr^{-2}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N}.\n\\label{eq:error-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-342.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-pairing",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly for all $\\lambda'\\le\\lambda$, $$\\label{eq:smoothed-mass}\n\\|S_rP'\\|_2\\le Cr^{-1},$$ and, writing $d=d_g(x,y)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\left\\langle S_rP,*S_rP'\\right\\rangle_{L^2}\n&\\ge cr^{-4}\\iint_{d<r}\n|\\ell-\\tau_{yx}m|^2\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)\\,d\\lambda'(y,m)\n\\nonumber\\\\\n&\\quad-Cr^{-2}\\iint(1+d/r)^{-6}\\,d\\mu(x)\\,d\\mu'(y).\n\\label{eq:positive-pairing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover $$\\label{eq:shell}\nr^{-2}\\int_X(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-6}\\,d\\mu(x)\\le C.$$",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold. Suppose that $$P(\\alpha)=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)$$ for a finite positive measure $\\lambda$, with projection $\\mu$. Suppose also that $Q\\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current. Assume the estimates $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\mu(B_r(x))&\\le Cr^2,\\label{eq:abstract-growth}\\\\\n\\iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}|\\ell-\\tau_{yx}m|^2\n\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(y,m)&\\le Cr^4\n\\label{eq:abstract-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently small $r$, uniformly in $x$. Then the limit $$\\theta(x)=\\lim_{r\\downarrow0}r^{-2}\\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point. If $E=\\{x:\\theta(x)>0\\}$, the current $P_E=\\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.",
        "source_id": "PM-342.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold. Assume the estimates $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\mu(B_r(x))&\\le Cr^2,\\label{eq:abstract-growth}\\\\\n\\iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}|\\ell-\\tau_{yx}m|^2\n\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(y,m)&\\le Cr^4\n\\label{eq:abstract-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently small $r$, uniformly in $x$. Then the limit $$\\theta(x)=\\lim_{r\\downarrow0}r^{-2}\\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point. If $E=\\{x:\\theta(x)>0\\}$, the current $P_E=\\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.",
          "source_id": "PM-342.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold.           "exact_quote": "Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold. Suppose also that $Q\\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current.         "exact_quote": "On $X=\\mathbb R^4/\\mathbb Z^4$, use the Euclidean metric and standard orientation, and put $e^{ij}=dx_i\\wedge dx_j$. Set $$U=e^{12}+e^{34},\\qquad V_0=e^{13}-e^{24},\\qquad\n W=e^{14}+e^{23},$$ $$f=\\tfrac14\\sin(2\\pi x_1),\\qquad\n k=\\tfrac14\\sin(2\\pi x_2),\\qquad\n r=(1+f^2+k^2)^{1/2}.$$ The form $F=(U+fV_0+kW)/r$ is self-dual and has squared norm two, so it is the fundamental form of a smooth orthogonal almost complex structure $J$ of this orientation. The form $$\\eta=U+2f e^{13}+2k e^{23}$$ is closed. Its self-dual part is $rF$, so it is invariant, and its anti-self-dual part is $$f(e^{13}+e^{24})+k(e^{23}-e^{14}).$$ Its Hermitian eigenvalues are $r\\pm\\sqrt{f^2+k^2}>0$. Thus $\\eta$ is compatible. The sine terms are exact, giving $[\\eta]=[U]$.\n\nA closed anti-invariant form is self-dual and harmonic for the flat metric, hence is a constant combination $aU+bV_0+cW$. Orthogonality to $F$ gives $a+bf+ck=0$ everywhere. Varying $x_1,x_2$ yields $a=b=c=0$; thus $H_J^-=0$. The closed forms $U\\pm V_0$ have invariant projections $(1\\pm f)F/r$, so both tame $J$. Their classes pair to zero, since $U^2=V_0^2$. Neither class can have a compatible representative, which would wedge strictly positively with a tamer in the other class. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Symplectic form** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A smooth real two-form $\omega$ is symplectic if it is nondegenerate and closed, meaning $d\omega=0$.
- **Taming form** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A real two-form $\omega$ *tames* $J$ if $\omega(v,Jv)>0$ for every nonzero tangent vector $v$.
- **Compatible form** ([source](#pm-h-2)): It is *compatible* with $J$ if, in addition, $\omega(Ju,Jv)=\omega(u,v)$.
- **Taming and compatible cohomology cones** ([source](#stmt-thm-taming-cones)): Let $\mathcal K_J^t,\mathcal K_J^c\subset H^2(X;\mathbb R)$ be the classes represented by taming and compatible symplectic forms, respectively.
- **Anti-invariant cohomology** ([source](#stmt-thm-taming-cones)): Let $H_J^-$ be the subspace represented by smooth closed real anti-invariant two-forms.
- **Invariant slice of the taming cone** ([source](#stmt-thm-taming-cones)): Set $$\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\perp,\qquad \mathcal C=\mathcal K_J^t\cap\mathcal V,$$ where orthogonality is for the intersection pairing.
- **Unit complex-line bivectors** ([source](#sec:currents)): Let $\mathscr C\to X$ be the bundle of unit complex line bivectors $$\mathscr C_x=\{v\wedge Jv:\ \|v\|_g=1\}.$$
- **Closed two-current** ([source](#sec:currents)): A two-current acts on smooth two-forms. It is *closed* if it annihilates exact two-forms.
- **Trace measure** ([source](#stmt-prop-separation)): The projection $\mu$ of $\lambda$ to $X$ is the *trace measure* of $P$: for every smooth function $b$, $P(bF)=\int_Xb\,d\mu$.
- **Hodge resolvent regularizer** ([source](#sec:energy)): Let $\Delta=dd^*+d^*d$ be the Hodge Laplacian, and set $$S_r=(1+r^2\Delta)^{-3},\qquad 0<r<r_0.$$
- **Two-density** ([source](#stmt-thm-splitting)): Then the limit $$\theta(x)=\lim_{r\downarrow0}r^{-2}\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point.
- **Positive-density restriction** ([source](#stmt-thm-splitting)): If $E=\{x:\theta(x)>0\}$, the current $P_E=\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.

Suppose that $$P(\\alpha)=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)$$ for a finite positive measure $\\lambda$, with projection $\\mu$. Suppose also that $Q\\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current. Assume the estimates $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\mu(B_r(x))&\\le Cr^2,\\label{eq:abstract-growth}\\\\\n\\iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}|\\ell-\\tau_{yx}m|^2\n\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(y,m)&\\le Cr^4\n\\label{eq:abstract-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently small $r$, uniformly in $x$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift): Elliptic generalized inverse and harmonic projection. -> A closed lift with the prescribed projection.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-separation): Separation of the positive cone from closed invariant forms; positive measure representation and lift correction. -> A positive separator and an anti-invariant correction.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-radial-correction-2): Corrected logarithmic and square-root radial tests. -> Uniform quadratic trace growth.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-energy): Resolvent kernel positivity, relative projection estimate, and vanishing closed-current pairing. -> Square-integrability, angular control, and vanishing mixed terms.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density): Radial comparison and almost-monotonicity. -> Finite density at every point.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse): Constant-polarization tangent measures, translation invariance, and dominated convergence. -> Vanishing transverse moment.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-2): Smooth density cutoffs; product of angular and transverse errors; successive dominated-convergence limits. -> Closedness of the positive-density restriction.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-3): Pair the separator with its zero-density residual, remove the correction, and contradict strict positivity on a closed tamer. -> Existence of a compatible symplectic form.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-taming-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-general-residual-2): Prescribed-class separation, cohomological pairings, residual estimates, and strict cone duality. -> Decomposition of the taming cone and characterization of compatible classes.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cor-taming-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-general-residual-3): Positive-definite anti-invariant subspace and the positive cone of the remaining intersection form. -> Equality of the invariant taming slice and the compatible cone.

Suppose that $$P(\\alpha)=\\int_{\\mathscr C}\\alpha_x(\\ell)\\,d\\lambda(x,\\ell)$$ for a finite positive measure $\\lambda$, with projection $\\mu$. Suppose also that $Q\\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current. Then the limit $$\\theta(x)=\\lim_{r\\downarrow0}r^{-2}\\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point. If $E=\\{x:\\theta(x)>0\\}$, the current $P_E=\\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.",

Let $\\mathcal K_J^t,\\mathcal K_J^c\\subset H^2(X;\\mathbb R)$ be the classes represented by taming and compatible symplectic forms, respectively. Let $H_J^-$ be the subspace represented by smooth closed real anti-invariant two-forms. Set $$\\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\\perp,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C=\\mathcal K_J^t\\cap\\mathcal V,$$ where orthogonality is for the intersection pairing. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal K_J^t&=\\mathcal C+H_J^-,\\label{eq:tame-decomposition}\\\\\n \\mathcal K_J^c&=\n \\{a\\in\\mathcal C:\\ a\\cdot y>0\n   \\text{ for every }0\\ne y\\in\\overline{\\mathcal C}\\}.\n \\label{eq:compatible-duality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The closure is taken in $\\mathcal V$, and the sum in <a href=\"#eq:tame-decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tame-decomposition\">[eq:tame-decomposition]</a> is a Minkowski sum.",

If $h_J^-=b_2^+(X)-1$, then $$\\mathcal K_J^t=\\mathcal K_J^c+H_J^-.$$ In particular, if $b_2^+(X)=1$, then $\\mathcal K_J^t=\\mathcal K_J^c$: every taming class has a smooth compatible symplectic representative.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **compatible-existence**: The given almost complex structure admits a smooth compatible symplectic form.
- **positive-density-closure**: The density exists at every point and the restriction $P_E$ to positive-density centers is closed.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **prescribed-class**: Preserve an arbitrary taming cohomology class while constructing a compatible form. -> Existence does not ensure that the specified class has a compatible representative.
- **pointwise-projection**: Produce a symplectic compatible form by the elementary invariant projection of a tamer. -> The projection generally loses closedness.
- **unconditional-restriction**: Restrict an arbitrary positive current to its positive-density set and claim closedness. -> The stated closedness result requires the correction, mass bound and angular bound.
- **finite-scale-proof**: Certify density splitting from regularizations at finitely many scales. -> Finite scale observations do not establish the limiting density or vanishing cutoff errors.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The existence result uses separation in spaces of smooth forms, an elliptic lift, distributional currents and successive limits. The density-splitting result requires estimates at all sufficiently small scales. The source gives no finite input representation or terminating construction for these results; the explicit torus example illustrates the distinction between existence and prescribed classes.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The existence result requires a smooth almost complex structure on a closed connected smooth real four-manifold and a taming symplectic form. Blocked use: ['Applying the existence conclusion without the smoothness, closedness, dimension, or taming hypotheses.']
- **L2** (edge_case): Existence of a compatible form does not preserve an arbitrary taming cohomology class. Blocked use: ['Replacing a taming form by a compatible form while claiming that its class is automatically fixed.']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): The four-torus example separates the taming and compatible cones for prescribed classes; it still admits a compatible form. Blocked use: ['Treating strict cone inclusion as a counterexample to the existence theorem.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The compatible-cone criterion uses strict intersection positivity against every nonzero class in the closure of the invariant part of the taming cone. Blocked use: ['Replacing strict positivity by nonnegative intersection tests or checking only selected classes.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The cone identity in the corollary depends on the stated anti-invariant cohomology dimension condition. Blocked use: ['Asserting the corollary cone identity for every tamed structure.']
- **L6** (nonconstructive): The existence proof proceeds by separation and contradiction; it does not specify a finite construction of a compatible form from a taming form. Blocked use: ['Claiming a terminating implementation or a computational complexity bound for producing a compatible form.']
- **L7** (approximation): The elementary invariant projection preserves positivity on complex lines but can lose closedness. Blocked use: ['Using the elementary projection alone to produce a compatible symplectic form.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Density splitting requires closedness of the corrected current, an anti-invariant correction in the stated integrability space, and both mass and angular estimates. Blocked use: ['Restricting a positive current to its positive-density set without checking the corrected-current and estimate hypotheses.']
- **L9** (approximation): The tangent-measure argument gives qualitative transverse decay without a quantitative convergence rate. Blocked use: ['Assigning a numerical convergence rate or finite stopping tolerance to the density restriction proof.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The projection leakage estimate fixes a smooth orthogonal bundle projection and allows its constant to depend on that projection. Blocked use: ['Assuming uniform leakage control for projections that vary with the regularization scale.']
- **L11** (approximation): The density-cutoff limits are successive and do not require a derivative bound uniform in the cutoff index. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the cutoff limits without an additional justification.']
- **L12** (edge_case): The existence theorem does not resolve the distinct prescribed-volume a priori estimate conjecture. Blocked use: ['Claiming prescribed-volume estimates from the tamed-to-compatible existence conclusion.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): The regularized pairing estimates use self-duality of anti-invariant forms in the stated dimension. Blocked use: ['Transferring these pairing estimates to other dimensions without proving the required algebra.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every smooth almost complex structure on a closed four-manifold which is tamed by a symplectic form is compatible with a symplectic form. This gives a positive solution to Donaldson's tamed-to-compatible conjecture. We also characterize the compatible cone by strict intersection positivity on the closure of the invariant part of the taming cone.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Let $X$ be a closed smooth four-manifold and let $J$ be a smooth almost complex structure on $X$. A smooth real two-form $\omega$ is symplectic if it is nondegenerate and closed, meaning $d\omega=0$. A real two-form $\omega$ *tames* $J$ if $\omega(v,Jv)>0$ for every nonzero tangent vector $v$. It is *compatible* with $J$ if, in addition, $\omega(Ju,Jv)=\omega(u,v)$. A taming form is automatically nondegenerate; thus a closed taming form is symplectic.

Donaldson asked whether the existence of a taming symplectic form on a closed four-manifold implies the existence of a compatible one (Donaldson 2006, sec. 5.2, Question 2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Donaldson2006](#ref-Donaldson2006)]. This existence question is distinct from his a priori estimate conjecture for a prescribed-volume equation. The elementary projection $\omega\mapsto \frac12(\omega+\omega(J\,\cdot,J\,\cdot))$ shows the obstacle: it preserves positivity on complex lines but generally loses closedness. Our main result answers the existence question affirmatively.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Suppose that a smooth almost complex structure $J$ on a closed connected smooth real four-manifold $X$ is tamed by a symplectic form. Then there is a smooth symplectic form compatible with $J$.*

</div>

The almost complex structure in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the given one. The conclusion imposes no condition on the cohomology class of the compatible form. In particular, it does not assert that an arbitrary taming class contains a compatible representative.

There is also an intrinsic description of the compatible cone in terms of the taming cone and the intersection pairing.

<a id="stmt-thm-taming-cones"></a>
<div id="thm:taming-cones" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:tame-decomposition"></a>
<a id="eq:compatible-duality"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Taming and compatible cones). *Let $X,J$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and give $X$ the orientation induced by $J$. Let $\mathcal K_J^t,\mathcal K_J^c\subset H^2(X;\mathbb R)$ be the classes represented by taming and compatible symplectic forms, respectively. Let $H_J^-$ be the subspace represented by smooth closed real anti-invariant two-forms. Set $$\mathcal V=(H_J^-)^\perp,\qquad
 \mathcal C=\mathcal K_J^t\cap\mathcal V,$$ where orthogonality is for the intersection pairing. Then $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal K_J^t&=\mathcal C+H_J^-,\label{eq:tame-decomposition}\\
 \mathcal K_J^c&=
 \{a\in\mathcal C:\ a\cdot y>0
   \text{ for every }0\ne y\in\overline{\mathcal C}\}.
 \label{eq:compatible-duality}
\end{aligned}$$ The closure is taken in $\mathcal V$, and the sum in <a href="#eq:tame-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tame-decomposition">[eq:tame-decomposition]</a> is a Minkowski sum.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-taming-cones"></a>
<div id="cor:taming-cones" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3**. *Under the same hypotheses, write $h_J^-=\dim H_J^-$ and let $b_2^+(X)$ be the positive index of the intersection form. If $h_J^-=b_2^+(X)-1$, then $$\mathcal K_J^t=\mathcal K_J^c+H_J^-.$$ In particular, if $b_2^+(X)=1$, then $\mathcal K_J^t=\mathcal K_J^c$: every taming class has a smooth compatible symplectic representative.*

</div>

The proofs are given in Section <a href="#sec:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cones">8</a>. They use separation in a prescribed cohomology class and the current estimates below. The inclusion $\mathcal K_J^c+H_J^-\subseteq\mathcal K_J^t$ can be strict when $h_J^-<b_2^+-1$, even if $H_J^-=0$; Example <a href="#ex:strict-cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ex:strict-cones">21</a> gives an explicit four-torus.

The integrable case belongs to the theory of compact complex surfaces. Buchdahl (Buchdahl 1999, Theorem 11) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Buchdahl1999](#ref-Buchdahl1999)] and Lamari (Lamari 1999, Corollary 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Lamari1999](#ref-Lamari1999)] gave classification-free proofs that even first Betti number implies the existence of a Kähler metric. Lamari’s positive exact current on a surface with odd first Betti number also obstructs a taming form (Lamari 1999, Theorem 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Lamari1999](#ref-Lamari1999)]. Li and Zhang proved the equivalence between the existence of taming and compatible symplectic forms on complex surfaces (Li and Zhang 2009, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LiZhang2009](#ref-LiZhang2009)].

The pseudoholomorphic-curve approach has a different starting point. For structures on $\mathbb{CP}^2$ tamed by the standard symplectic form, Gromov constructed a compatible form by averaging currents of pseudoholomorphic lines (Gromov 1985, sec. 2.4.A$'$) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Gromov1985](#ref-Gromov1985)]. Taubes developed integration over curve moduli spaces to obtain compatibility for a residual subset of the smooth almost complex structures tamed by a fixed symplectic form when $b_2^+=1$ (Taubes 2011, 2017) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Taubes2011](#ref-Taubes2011), [Taubes2017](#ref-Taubes2017)]. Theorem 1 of (Taubes 2011) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Taubes2011](#ref-Taubes2011)] also states preservation of the taming form’s cohomology class when that class is rational. Li and Zhang extended this approach to every tamed structure on $S^2\times S^2$ and $\mathbb{CP}^2\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}{}^2$, and to further rational manifolds under hypotheses on embedded pseudoholomorphic spheres (Li and Zhang 2015, Theorems 1.2 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[LiZhang2015](#ref-LiZhang2015)].

An invariant two-form satisfies $\alpha(Ju,Jv)=\alpha(u,v)$; an anti-invariant one satisfies $\alpha(Ju,Jv)=-\alpha(u,v)$. Write $h_J^-=\dim H_J^-$, where $H_J^-$ is the anti-invariant cohomology space defined in Theorem <a href="#thm:taming-cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:taming-cones">2</a>. The condition $h_J^-=b_2^+-1$ occurs in Buchdahl-type approaches (Tan et al. 2022; Lin and Zhou 2025) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[TWZZ2022](#ref-TWZZ2022), [LinZhou2025](#ref-LinZhou2025)], in the compatibility criteria of Wang, Wang and Zhu (H. Wang, Wang, and Zhu 2023, Theorems 4.3 and 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[WangWangZhu2023](#ref-WangWangZhu2023)], and in the taming corollary of Wang, Zhang, Zheng and Zhu (K. Wang et al. 2025, Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[WangZhangZhengZhu2025](#ref-WangZhangZhengZhu2025)]. The latter paper’s generalized Monge–Ampère theorem starts with an almost Kähler structure. Li and Ning’s recent study of spaces of Kähler and holomorphically tamed forms allows the integrable complex structure to vary (Li and Ning 2026) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[LiNing2026](#ref-LiNing2026)].

The current approach has a classical cone-duality background. Sullivan related closed forms positive on a field of tangent directions to the corresponding structure currents (Sullivan 1976) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Sullivan1976](#ref-Sullivan1976)]. Harvey and Lawson characterized Kähler manifolds through positive currents (Harvey and Lawson 1983) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HarveyLawson1983](#ref-HarveyLawson1983)]; Li and Zhang developed the almost-complex formulation used to compare taming and compatibility (Li and Zhang 2009, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LiZhang2009](#ref-LiZhang2009)]. In the argument below, separation gives a positive functional vanishing on closed invariant forms. An explicit elliptic lift and quantitative current estimates connect this obstruction to the existence of a taming form.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The argument and its main estimate

Fix an auxiliary $J$-Hermitian metric. If no compatible form exists, separation produces a nonzero positive current $P$ that annihilates closed invariant forms. An elliptic correction gives $T=P+Q$, which annihilates every closed two-form, with $Q$ initially an anti-invariant distribution. Represent $P$ as a positive weighted average of unit complex-line bivectors, and let $\mu$ be the resulting measure on $X$, its trace measure. Corrected radial test functions give $\mu(B_r(x))\le Cr^2$. Regularization by a finite power of the Hodge resolvent then proves $Q\in L^2$ and an angular estimate: over pairs of complex lines in this representation whose base points are less than $r$ apart, the integral of their squared difference, after parallel transport, is $O(r^4)$.

The first regularized pairing only bounds $\|Q\|_2$, because the negative error in this pairing is only uniformly bounded. To remove that error, we distinguish the points where $P$ has positive two-dimensional density. Write $$\theta(x)=\lim_{r\downarrow0}r^{-2}\mu(B_r(x)),
\qquad E=\{x:\theta(x)>0\}.$$ Existence of the limit is part of the argument. The principal step, Theorem <a href="#thm:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:splitting">13</a>, proves that the restriction $P_E=\mathbf 1_E P$ is closed. It applies to any closed current $P+Q$ with an anti-invariant $L^2$ correction and the stated mass and angular bounds; annihilation of all closed forms is not needed for this step.

The reason the angular estimate suffices is a balance between two errors. Planar tangent measures show that, over the same nearby pairs, the squared normal component of the displacement between base points, relative to the first complex line and divided by $r^2$, has integral $o(r^2)$. Closedness of $P+Q$ trades tangential derivatives of a smooth density cutoff for a product of angular and transverse errors. The derivative and normalization of the cutoff contribute $r^{-3}$. Cauchy–Schwarz therefore bounds the decisive boundary term by $$r^{-3}\,O(r^2)\,o(r)=o(1),$$ where the two factors are the square roots of the angular and transverse moments. The $L^2$ bound controls the correction’s cutoff error separately. Together these estimates prove closedness of the density restriction without requiring a quantitative rate for tangent measure convergence.

Once $P_E$ is closed, the residual $T-P_E=\mathbf 1_{X\setminus E}P+Q$ is closed as well. Its positive summand is concentrated on zero-density centers, where the negative error in the pairing does tend to zero. Pairing this residual with $T$, and using the vanishing of the mixed terms, forces $Q=0$. The resulting nonzero positive current $P=T$ cannot annihilate a closed taming form.

The relative projection estimate in Lemma <a href="#lem:mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:mixing">11</a>, for fixed smooth orthogonal bundle projections, and the closedness statement in Theorem <a href="#thm:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:splitting">13</a> may be useful separately. The regularized pairing estimates use the four-dimensional fact that anti-invariant two-forms are self-dual. Sections <a href="#sec:currents" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:currents">2</a> and <a href="#sec:mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mass">3</a> construct the separating current and prove its mass bound. Section <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">4</a> establishes the regularization and angular estimates. Section <a href="#sec:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:density">5</a> proves density existence and the planar tangent description; Section <a href="#sec:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:splitting">6</a> uses them to control cutoff boundaries and prove closedness of $P_E$. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">7</a> completes the existence argument. Section <a href="#sec:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cones">8</a> develops the prescribed-class separation and proves the cone description.

A triple of closed two-forms on an oriented four-manifold is *hypersymplectic* if every nonzero real linear combination is symplectic and induces the given orientation. The companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[OpenAIHypersymplectic2026](#ref-OpenAIHypersymplectic2026)] combines the current estimates above with additional prescribed-class and deformation arguments to deform hypersymplectic triples normalized by $\int_X\omega_i\wedge\omega_j=\delta_{ij}$ on closed connected oriented smooth four-manifolds to hyperkähler triples, while fixing each class $[\omega_i]\in H^2(X;\mathbb R)$.

<a id="sec:currents"></a>
### A current obtained by separation

We first construct the current that would obstruct compatibility: a positive current and an anti-invariant correction whose sum annihilates every closed two-form.

Give $X$ its complex orientation. Fix a smooth $J$-Hermitian metric $g$, and write $$F(u,v)=g(Ju,v),\qquad
(R\alpha)(u,v)=\tfrac12\bigl(\alpha(u,v)-\alpha(Ju,Jv)\bigr).$$ Thus $R$ is the orthogonal projection onto the anti-invariant two-forms, and $1-R$ projects onto the invariant two-forms. All forms and currents are real.

<a id="eq:algebra"></a>
We identify bivectors with two-forms using $g$. Let $\mathscr C\to X$ be the bundle of unit complex line bivectors $$\mathscr C_x=\{v\wedge Jv:\ |v|_g=1\}.$$ For $\ell\in\mathscr C_x$, let $L_\ell\subset T_xX$ be its oriented plane. The following pointwise identities will be used: $$\label{eq:algebra}
\operatorname{im}R\subset\Lambda_g^+,\qquad
*F=F,\qquad |F|^2=2,\qquad
|\ell|=1,\qquad \ell^+=F/2,\qquad F(\ell)=1.$$ They follow by taking a unitary frame $(v,Jv,w,Jw)$. In that frame $F$ corresponds to $v\wedge Jv+w\wedge Jw$, and the anti-invariant subspace is spanned by $$v\wedge w-Jv\wedge Jw,\qquad
Jv\wedge w+v\wedge Jw.$$

A two-current acts on smooth two-forms. It is *closed* if it annihilates exact two-forms. Under the $L^2$ identification with distributional two-forms, this means $d^*T=0$, or equivalently $d(*T)=0$. Multiplication and orthogonal projections on distributional forms act on currents by duality.

<a id="stmt-lem-lift"></a>
<div id="lem:lift" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (A closed lift). *There is a real order-zero pseudodifferential operator $$B:C^\infty(\operatorname{im}R)\longrightarrow\Omega^2(X)$$ such that $dB=0$ and $RB=\mathrm{id}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lift"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lift</summary>

*Proof.* Consider $\mathcal L=Rdd^*$ on sections of $\operatorname{im}R$. If $\xi\ne0$, the projection of $\xi\wedge\iota_{\xi^\sharp}$ from self-dual two-forms back to self-dual two-forms is $|\xi|^2/2$ times the identity. By <a href="#eq:algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra">[eq:algebra]</a>, the principal symbol of $\mathcal L$ on $\operatorname{im}R$ is therefore the same scalar. In particular $\mathcal L$ is elliptic. For anti-invariant smooth forms $a,b$, $$\left\langle \mathcal La,b\right\rangle_{L^2}=\left\langle d^*a,d^*b\right\rangle_{L^2}.$$ It is nonnegative and self-adjoint. Every element of its kernel is smooth and coclosed; being self-dual, it is also closed.

Let $H$ be the orthogonal projection onto this kernel and let $G$ be the inverse of $\mathcal L$ on its orthogonal complement, extended by zero on the kernel. The elliptic generalized-inverse theorem gives $G$ of order $-2$ and $H$ a smoothing finite-rank operator; see (Melrose 2007, Proposition 6.4 and Theorem 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[MelroseIML](#ref-MelroseIML)]. Set $$B=dd^*G+H.$$ Then $dB=0$, while $RB=\mathcal LG+H=\mathrm{id}$. ◻

</details>

The projected elliptic operator used here also appears in Lejmi’s proof of local compatibility in dimension four (Lejmi 2006, proof of Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Lejmi2006](#ref-Lejmi2006)]. Drăghici and Zhang’s exact-form result gives the corresponding global linear correction phenomenon (Draghici and Zhang 2012, Proposition 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[DraghiciZhang2012](#ref-DraghiciZhang2012)]. The present formula specifies the closed lift and its mapping properties; positivity remains a separate problem.

<a id="stmt-prop-separation"></a>
<div id="prop:separation" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:positive-representation"></a>
<a id="eq:annihilation"></a>
**Proposition 5**. *If $J$ admits no compatible symplectic form, there are currents $P,Q,T$ and a finite positive measure $\lambda$ on $\mathscr C$ such that <a id="eq:decomposition"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
P(\alpha)&=\int_{\mathscr C}\alpha_x(\ell)\,d\lambda(x,\ell),
&\lambda(\mathscr C)&=1,\label{eq:positive-representation}\\
T&=P+Q,
&RP&=0,\quad RQ=Q,\quad *Q=Q,\label{eq:decomposition}\\
T(\alpha)&=0&&\text{for every smooth closed two-form }\alpha.
\label{eq:annihilation}
\end{aligned}$$ The current $P$ annihilates every closed invariant two-form. The projection $\mu$ of $\lambda$ to $X$ is the *trace measure* of $P$: for every smooth function $b$, $P(bF)=\int_Xb\,d\mu$. Each of $P,Q,T$ belongs to $H^{-3}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-separation"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-separation</summary>

*Proof.* In the Fréchet space of smooth invariant two-forms, the cone of positive definite forms is open and convex. It is disjoint from the linear subspace of closed invariant forms by hypothesis. Hahn–Banach separation gives a nonzero continuous functional nonnegative on the cone and zero on that subspace. Extend it to all two-forms by composing with $1-R$, and call it $P$. This is the positive-current separation framework of Sullivan and Harvey–Lawson (Sullivan 1976; Harvey and Lawson 1983) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Sullivan1976](#ref-Sullivan1976), [HarveyLawson1983](#ref-HarveyLawson1983)]; for its almost-complex formulation, see (Li and Zhang 2009, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[LiZhang2009](#ref-LiZhang2009)].

Positivity extends to semidefinite invariant forms by adding $\varepsilon F$ and taking $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. Since $C\|\alpha\|_\infty F\pm(1-R)\alpha$ are semidefinite for a fixed norm-comparison constant, $$|P(\alpha)|\le C\|\alpha\|_\infty P(F).$$ Thus $P$ has order zero, and $P(F)>0$ because $P\ne0$. Scale $P$ so that $P(F)=1$.

For completeness, the measure representation can be obtained in local unitary frames. Invariant two-forms identify with Hermitian symmetric forms by $\alpha\mapsto\alpha(\,\cdot,J\,\cdot)$. The positive functional has a positive Hermitian matrix of coefficient measures. Its trace is a positive measure $\mu$; the coefficient measures are absolutely continuous with respect to $\mu$. Their Radon–Nikodym density is a positive semidefinite Hermitian matrix of trace one, or equivalently a convex combination of bivectors in $\mathscr C_x$. Measurable spectral decomposition in measurable unitary frames gives $\lambda$ in <a href="#eq:positive-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-representation">[eq:positive-representation]</a>. The identity $F(\ell)=1$ shows that its projection is $\mu$ and that its total mass is one.

Define $$T(\alpha)=P(\alpha-BR\alpha),\qquad
Q(\alpha)=-P(BR\alpha).$$ For closed $\alpha$, the form $\alpha-BR\alpha$ is closed and invariant, proving <a href="#eq:annihilation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:annihilation">[eq:annihilation]</a>. Moreover $RP=0$ and $RQ=Q$, so $*Q=Q$ by <a href="#eq:algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra">[eq:algebra]</a>. In particular $T$ is a closed current.

A finite measure on a compact four-manifold defines an element of $H^{-3}$, by the Sobolev embedding $H^3\subset C^0$. The order-zero operator $RB^*$ is bounded on this Sobolev space by the pseudodifferential Sobolev mapping theorem (Taylor 1991, Proposition 0.5.E) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Taylor1991](#ref-Taylor1991)], so the same conclusion holds for $Q=-RB^*P$ and $T$. ◻

</details>

Hereafter $C$, with or without a subscript, denotes a finite constant independent of the small scale parameters. It may change from one occurrence to the next. Constants are uniform over centers in $X$.

<a id="sec:mass"></a>
### Quadratic mass growth

Let $P,\mu$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:separation">5</a>. We use the annihilation of closed invariant forms to bound the mass of $P$ in every small ball. Write $B_s(x)$ for a geodesic ball.

<a id="stmt-prop-growth"></a>
<div id="prop:growth" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:growth"></a>
**Proposition 6**. *There is a constant $C$ such that $$\label{eq:growth}
\mu(B_s(x))\le Cs^2$$ for all $x\in X$ and $s>0$.*

</div>

The proof uses closed invariant test forms $$Du=(1-BR)dd_J^cu,\qquad d_J^cu=-du\circ J.$$ The Hessian terms of $dd_J^cu$, with $J$ frozen at a point, form an invariant two-form. Consequently $Rdd_J^c$ is a first-order differential operator on functions.

<a id="eq:scaled-input"></a>
We first spell out the local estimate needed for the order-zero correction. In dimension four, an order-zero pseudodifferential operator has an off-diagonal kernel bounded by $C|y-z|^{-4}$ in local coordinates (Taylor 1991, vol. 100, sec. 0.2, Proposition 0.2.B) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Taylor1991](#ref-Taylor1991)]. Work in a fixed smooth local bundle trivialization. If $f$ is supported in a coordinate ball of radius $a\le1$ and its coefficient derivatives satisfy $$\label{eq:scaled-input}
|\partial^jf|\le C_jMa^{-j},$$ then its local contribution to the operator is bounded by $CM$. Indeed, write $f(z)=M\widetilde f((z-z_0)/a)$. Six bounded derivatives of $\widetilde f$ give $|\widehat{\widetilde f}(\xi)|\le C(1+|\xi|^2)^{-3}$ by integration by parts. This bound is integrable in four dimensions, so $\|\widehat{\widetilde f}\|_1\le C$, and hence $\|\widehat f\|_1\le CM$. The bounded-symbol Fourier formula gives the assertion; smoothing remainders are controlled by $\|f\|_1$. This estimate uses the derivative bounds <a href="#eq:scaled-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaled-input">[eq:scaled-input]</a>, not general $L^\infty$ boundedness.

<a id="stmt-lem-radial-correction"></a>
<div id="lem:radial-correction" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:correction-bounds"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *Choose normal coordinates centered at $x$, on a uniformly small fixed ball, and put $t=d_g(x,y)$. Let a smooth radial cutoff be one on a smaller ball and vanish outside the coordinate ball. For $s>0$ small, let $u_s,h_s$ be this cutoff times $$\log\sqrt{s^2+t^2},\qquad \sqrt{s^2+t^2},$$ respectively, extended by zero. Near the center, $$\label{eq:correction-bounds}
|BRdd_J^cu_s|\le \frac{C}{s+t},
\qquad
|BRdd_J^ch_s|\le C(1+|\log(s+t)|).$$ Both expressions are uniformly bounded at any fixed positive distance from $x$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-radial-correction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-radial-correction</summary>

*Proof.* The first-order property of $Rdd_J^c$ gives $$|\partial^j(Rdd_J^cu_s)|\le C_j(s+t)^{-1-j},\qquad
|\partial^j(Rdd_J^ch_s)|\le C_j(s+t)^{-j}$$ near $x$. Away from $x$, all fixed-order derivatives are uniformly bounded, including terms from the cutoff.

At $y$, put $a=s+t$, assuming $a$ small. Localize the input to a ball about $y$ of radius a small fixed multiple of $a$. On this ball $s+d_g(x,\cdot)$ is comparable to $a$, so <a href="#eq:scaled-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaled-input">[eq:scaled-input]</a> gives contributions $C/a$ and $C$. For the rest of the input within distance $O(a)$ of $x$, the kernel is bounded by $Ca^{-4}$. The respective input $L^1$ bounds on this region are $Ca^3$ and $Ca^4$, again giving $C/a$ and $C$.

On the more distant shells about $x$, with radius $\rho\ge Ca$, the kernel is $O(\rho^{-4})$, the input bounds are $C/\rho$ and $C$, and the shell volume is $O(\rho^4)$. Summation over dyadic shells gives $C/a$ and $C(1+|\log a|)$. The logarithmic input has uniformly bounded total $L^1$ norm, since its local integral is at most $C\int_0^{\rho_0}\rho^3/(s+\rho)\,d\rho\le C\rho_0^3$. The square-root input has bounded $L^1$ norm as well. Thus the remaining smooth kernel terms are bounded uniformly. The same decomposition gives uniform bounds when $y$ stays a fixed distance from the center. Compactness makes all chart and operator constants uniform in $x$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-radial-correction-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-radial-correction</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:growth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:growth">6</a>.* In the normal coordinates of Lemma <a href="#lem:radial-correction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:radial-correction">7</a>, let $J_0=J(x)$ be the constant complex structure, orthogonal for the Euclidean metric at the center. On the inner ball, the Hessians of the two radial functions are $$\frac{I}{s^2+t^2}-\frac{2zz^\top}{(s^2+t^2)^2},
\qquad
\frac{I}{\sqrt{s^2+t^2}}-\frac{zz^\top}{(s^2+t^2)^{3/2}}.$$ Since $(z\cdot v)^2+(z\cdot J_0v)^2\le t^2|v|^2$, their complex-line traces satisfy <a id="eq:log-trace"></a><a id="eq:sqrt-trace"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
dd^c_{J_0}u_s(v,J_0v)
&\ge\frac{2s^2|v|^2}{(s^2+t^2)^2},\label{eq:log-trace}\\
dd^c_{J_0}h_s(v,J_0v)
&\ge\frac{|v|^2}{\sqrt{s^2+t^2}}.\label{eq:sqrt-trace}
\end{aligned}$$ The first lower bound is nonnegative everywhere and at least $c/s^2$ for $t<s$ and $g$-unit $v$.

Replacing $J_0$ by $J(y)$ both in the differential expression and in the second argument costs at most $C/(s+t)$ for $u_s$ and $C$ for $h_s$. To see this, use $J(y)-J_0=O(t)$ on the Hessian terms and the bounded first derivatives of $J$ on the gradient terms. Euclidean and $g$-norms are uniformly comparable.

Together with <a href="#eq:correction-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:correction-bounds">[eq:correction-bounds]</a>, this proves, for $s+t$ sufficiently small, $$Du_s(v,Jv)\ge \frac{c}{s^2}\mathbf 1_{\{t<s\}}-\frac{C}{s+t},
\qquad
Dh_s(v,Jv)\ge \frac{c'}{s+t}$$ with $c,c'>0$. In the second estimate the logarithmic correction is absorbed by the positive inverse-distance term on a sufficiently small fixed neighborhood. Choose a fixed $A>0$ large enough to absorb the negative term in the first estimate. Outside that neighborhood all terms are uniformly bounded. We obtain globally $$D(u_s+Ah_s)(v,Jv)\ge
\frac{c}{s^2}\mathbf 1_{B_s(x)}-C'$$ for all unit $v$ and small $s$.

The form on the left is closed and invariant. Applying $P$, which annihilates it, and using $\mu(X)=1$, gives $0\ge cs^{-2}\mu(B_s(x))-C'$. This proves <a href="#eq:growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:growth">[eq:growth]</a> for small $s$. Increasing the constant handles all larger radii. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:energy"></a>
### Regularization and angular control

We now combine quadratic mass growth with regularized current pairings to prove that $Q\in L^2$ and to control the variation of nearby complex-line directions. These are the remaining quantitative inputs to the density-splitting theorem.

Let $\Delta=dd^*+d^*d$ be the Hodge Laplacian, and set $$S_r=(1+r^2\Delta)^{-3},\qquad 0<r<r_0.$$ The exponent is fixed. By Proposition <a href="#prop:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:separation">5</a>, the regularizations of $P,Q,T$ belong to $L^2$: for fixed $r$, $S_r$ has order $-6$ and maps $H^{-3}$ into $H^3$. The same is true for a positive current $P'$ represented by any $\lambda'\le\lambda$. Write $\mu'$ for the projection of $\lambda'$.

<a id="stmt-lem-zero-pairing"></a>
<div id="lem:zero-pairing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8**. *If $T'$ is a closed current with $S_rT'\in L^2$, then <a id="eq:zero-pairing"></a>$$\label{eq:zero-pairing}
\left\langle S_rT,*S_rT'\right\rangle_{L^2}=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-zero-pairing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-zero-pairing</summary>

*Proof.* The operator $S_r$ is self-adjoint, commutes with star in middle degree, and commutes across degrees with $d$ and $d^*$. It preserves smooth forms. Thus $S_rT$ annihilates every smooth closed form by <a href="#eq:annihilation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:annihilation">[eq:annihilation]</a>. Closedness of $T'$ as a current means $d^*T'=0$, so $*S_rT'$ is a distributionally closed $L^2$ form. Its heat regularizations are smooth closed forms and converge to it in $L^2$. The asserted pairing follows by continuity. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The kernel and its positive leading part

For nearby $x,y$, let $\tau_{yx}$ denote metric parallel transport along the short geodesic from $y$ to $x$.

<a id="stmt-lem-kernel"></a>
<div id="lem:kernel" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:p-upper"></a>
<a id="eq:p-lower"></a>
<a id="eq:error-kernel"></a>
**Lemma 9**. *The continuous kernel $K_r(x,y)$ of $S_r^2$ can be written $$K_r(x,y)=p_r(x,y)\tau_{yx}+E_r(x,y),$$ where $p_r\ge0$ is supported in a fixed neighborhood of the diagonal. For every fixed positive integer $N$, $$\begin{aligned}
0\le p_r(x,y)&\le C_Nr^{-4}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N},
\label{eq:p-upper}\\
p_r(x,y)&\ge cr^{-4}\qquad(d_g(x,y)<r),
\label{eq:p-lower}\\
|E_r(x,y)|&\le C_Nr^{-2}(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-N}.
\label{eq:error-kernel}
\end{aligned}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-kernel"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-kernel</summary>

<a id="eq:gamma"></a>
*Proof.* Spectral calculus gives $$\label{eq:gamma}
S_r^2=\frac1{\Gamma(6)}
\int_0^\infty e^{-s}s^5e^{-r^2s\Delta}\,ds.$$ The Hodge Laplacian is the Levi-Civita connection Laplacian on forms plus a zeroth-order curvature endomorphism. The leading term of its heat kernel, localized by a nonnegative diagonal cutoff, is $$\chi(x,y)(4\pi t)^{-2}e^{-d_g(x,y)^2/(4t)}
a(x,y)\tau_{yx},$$ where $a$ is a smooth positive scalar volume correction and $\chi=1$ sufficiently near the diagonal. For the leading coefficient, see the author version (Ludewig 2018, sec. 3, equation (3.4)) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Ludewig2018](#ref-Ludewig2018)]; for uniform short-time asymptotics and the global Gaussian bound, see (Ludewig 2019, Theorems 1.1 and 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Ludewig2019](#ref-Ludewig2019)].

<a id="eq:heat-remainder"></a>
Fix a small $t_0>0$. After subtracting the displayed term, the short-time remainder has the bound $$\label{eq:heat-remainder}
Ct^{-1}e^{-c_0d_g(x,y)^2/t}+C_Mt^M,\qquad 0<t<t_0,$$ for any prescribed integer $M$, with constants depending on $M$. Indeed, take a sufficiently long heat parametrix: the further localized coefficients gain at least one factor of $t$, and its final uniform remainder can be made $O(t^M)$. Off the diagonal the cutoff errors are of arbitrarily high order.

Integrating the leading term for $r^2s<t_0$ in <a href="#eq:gamma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gamma">[eq:gamma]</a> defines $p_r$. For each fixed $N$, $$e^{-c_0d^2/(r^2s)}
\le C_N(1+\sqrt{s})^N(1+d/r)^{-N}.$$ The resulting $s$-integrals converge: their powers at zero are $s^3$ for the leading term and $s^4$ for the Gaussian remainder, and $e^{-s}$ controls infinity. This gives <a href="#eq:p-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-upper">[eq:p-upper]</a> and the Gaussian part of <a href="#eq:error-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:error-kernel">[eq:error-kernel]</a>. Restricting to $1\le s\le2$ gives <a href="#eq:p-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-lower">[eq:p-lower]</a> for small $r$.

The smooth remainder in <a href="#eq:heat-remainder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:heat-remainder">[eq:heat-remainder]</a> integrates to $O(r^{2M})$. Since $X$ has bounded diameter, choosing $2M\ge N-2$ absorbs it into <a href="#eq:error-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:error-kernel">[eq:error-kernel]</a>. For $s\ge t_0/r^2$, the heat kernel is uniformly bounded, and the tail of the gamma weight is exponentially small. This proves all the bounds. Notice that increasing $N$ changes the parametrix length, not the fixed resolvent power. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:angular-identity"></a>
For unit complex line bivectors at $x,y$, respectively $\ell,m$, the algebra <a href="#eq:algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra">[eq:algebra]</a> gives the exact identity $$\label{eq:angular-identity}
\left\langle \ell,*\tau_{yx}m\right\rangle
=\tfrac12\left\langle F_x,\tau_{yx}F_y\right\rangle-\left\langle \ell,\tau_{yx}m\right\rangle
=\tfrac12|\ell-\tau_{yx}m|^2
 -\tfrac14|F_x-\tau_{yx}F_y|^2.$$ Here parallel transport is an isometry and commutes with star. In particular the negative term is $O(d_g(x,y)^2)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-positive-pairing"></a>
<div id="lem:positive-pairing" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:smoothed-mass"></a>
<a id="eq:positive-pairing"></a>
<a id="eq:shell"></a>
**Lemma 10**. *Uniformly for all $\lambda'\le\lambda$, $$\label{eq:smoothed-mass}
\|S_rP'\|_2\le Cr^{-1},$$ and, writing $d=d_g(x,y)$, $$\begin{aligned}
\left\langle S_rP,*S_rP'\right\rangle_{L^2}
&\ge cr^{-4}\iint_{d<r}
|\ell-\tau_{yx}m|^2\,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda'(y,m)
\nonumber\\
&\quad-Cr^{-2}\iint(1+d/r)^{-6}\,d\mu(x)\,d\mu'(y).
\label{eq:positive-pairing}
\end{aligned}$$ Moreover $$\label{eq:shell}
r^{-2}\int_X(1+d_g(x,y)/r)^{-6}\,d\mu(x)\le C.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-positive-pairing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-positive-pairing</summary>

*Proof.* The inner ball and the dyadic shells in Proposition <a href="#prop:growth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:growth">6</a> give <a href="#eq:shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shell">[eq:shell]</a>: the shell at distance comparable to $2^jr$ contributes at most $Cr^2\,2^{-4j}$ before division by $r^2$.

Self-adjointness and commutation with star give $$\left\langle S_rP,*S_rP'\right\rangle=
\iint\left\langle \ell,*K_r(x,y)m\right\rangle
\,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda'(y,m).$$ The identity is justified directly by the continuous kernel, or by approximation in $H^{-3}$: the operator $S_r^2$ has order $-12$ and maps $H^{-3}$ into $H^9$, so the dual pairings are continuous. Using <a href="#eq:angular-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:angular-identity">[eq:angular-identity]</a>, retain its nonnegative term only on $d<r$, where <a href="#eq:p-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-lower">[eq:p-lower]</a> applies. By <a href="#eq:p-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-upper">[eq:p-upper]</a> with $N\ge8$, the negative term is bounded by $Cr^{-2}(1+d/r)^{-6}$; the kernel error has the same bound. This proves <a href="#eq:positive-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-pairing">[eq:positive-pairing]</a>. For <a href="#eq:smoothed-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smoothed-mass">[eq:smoothed-mass]</a>, use the analogous identity for $\|S_rP'\|_2^2$, take absolute values of the kernel, and apply <a href="#eq:shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shell">[eq:shell]</a> and $\mu'\le\mu$. ◻

</details>

The kernel estimate and quadratic growth bound the negative error in <a href="#eq:positive-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-pairing">[eq:positive-pairing]</a> uniformly. To extract an $L^2$ bound for $Q$, we must also control the cross terms between invariant and anti-invariant components. The next estimate supplies this control.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Mixing of invariant and anti-invariant components

<a id="stmt-lem-mixing"></a>
<div id="lem:mixing" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:mixing"></a>
**Lemma 11** (Relative projection estimate). *Let $\Pi$ be a fixed smooth orthogonal projection on $\Lambda^2T^*X$. If a distributional two-form $W$ satisfies $\Pi W=0$ and $S_rW\in L^2$, then $$\label{eq:mixing}
\|\Pi S_rW\|_2\le Cr\|S_rW\|_2.$$ The constant may depend on $\Pi$, but is independent of $r$ and $W$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-mixing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-mixing</summary>

*Proof.* Put $U=S_rW$ and $A_r=(1+r^2\Delta)^3$. The operators $S_r$ and $A_r$ are inverse on distributions. Since $\Pi A_rU=\Pi W=0$, we have, distributionally, $$\Pi U=S_rA_r(\Pi U)=S_r[A_r,\Pi]U.$$ On smooth forms the adjoint of the operator on the right is $-[A_r,\Pi]S_r$. Expanding the commutator gives terms $\binom3j r^{2j}[\Delta^j,\Pi]S_r$, $1\le j\le3$. The principal symbol of $\Delta^j$ is scalar, so $[\Delta^j,\Pi]$ has differential order at most $2j-1$. The elliptic Sobolev calculus (Melrose 2007, Propositions 6.7–6.8) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[MelroseIML](#ref-MelroseIML)], together with spectral calculus for the Hodge Laplacian, gives $$\|S_r\|_{L^2\to H^{2j-1}}\le Cr^{-(2j-1)}.$$ Each term of the adjoint therefore has norm $O(r)$. Taking its bounded Hilbert-space adjoint extends $S_r[A_r,\Pi]$ to $L^2$, agreeing with its distributional action. Applying this bound to $U$ proves <a href="#eq:mixing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mixing">[eq:mixing]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-energy"></a>
<div id="prop:energy" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:cross-vanish"></a>
**Proposition 12**. *The correction $Q$ belongs to $L^2$, and <a id="eq:angular"></a>$$\label{eq:angular}
\mathcal I_r:=
\iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}|\ell-\tau_{yx}m|^2
\,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda(y,m)\le Cr^4.$$ For every positive current $P'$ represented by $\lambda'\le\lambda$, $$\label{eq:cross-vanish}
\lim_{r\downarrow0}\left\langle S_rP',*S_rQ\right\rangle_{L^2}=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-energy"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-energy</summary>

<a id="eq:cross-bound"></a>
*Proof.* For any anti-invariant distribution $V$ with $S_rV\in L^2$, split both factors along $R$ and $1-R$, and use Lemmas <a href="#lem:positive-pairing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:positive-pairing">10</a> and <a href="#lem:mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:mixing">11</a>: $$\label{eq:cross-bound}
|\left\langle S_rP',S_rV\right\rangle|
\le Cr\|S_rP'\|_2\|S_rV\|_2
\le C\|S_rV\|_2.$$ Since $Q$ is self-dual, Lemma <a href="#lem:zero-pairing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:zero-pairing">8</a> with $T'=T$ gives $$0=\left\langle S_rP,*S_rP\right\rangle
+2\left\langle S_rP,S_rQ\right\rangle+\|S_rQ\|_2^2.$$ Equations <a href="#eq:positive-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-pairing">[eq:positive-pairing]</a>, <a href="#eq:shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shell">[eq:shell]</a>, and <a href="#eq:cross-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-bound">[eq:cross-bound]</a> imply $$cr^{-4}\mathcal I_r+\|S_rQ\|_2^2
\le C+C\|S_rQ\|_2.$$ Thus $\|S_rQ\|_2$ is uniformly bounded and $\mathcal I_r\le Cr^4$. A weakly convergent $L^2$ subsequence, together with distributional convergence $S_rQ\to Q$, proves $Q\in L^2$.

For any anti-invariant $V\in L^2$, <a href="#eq:cross-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-bound">[eq:cross-bound]</a> and the $L^2$ contraction property of $S_r$ give a uniform bound by $C\|V\|_2$. If $V$ is smooth, then it is self-dual and $$\left\langle S_rP',*S_rV\right\rangle=P'(S_r^2V)\longrightarrow P'(V)=0,$$ because $S_r^2V\to V$ smoothly. Smooth anti-invariant forms are dense in the anti-invariant $L^2$ subspace: smooth first and then apply $R$. Approximation of $Q$ now proves <a href="#eq:cross-vanish" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-vanish">[eq:cross-vanish]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:density"></a>
### Densities and planar tangent measures

We isolate the geometric statement that will be used to finish the proof. In its hypotheses, $P$ need not annihilate closed invariant forms.

<a id="stmt-thm-splitting"></a>
<div id="thm:splitting" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:abstract-growth"></a>
<a id="eq:abstract-angular"></a>
**Theorem 13** (Closedness of the positive-density part). *Fix a smooth almost complex structure $J$ and a $J$-Hermitian metric on a closed four-manifold. Suppose that $$P(\alpha)=\int_{\mathscr C}\alpha_x(\ell)\,d\lambda(x,\ell)$$ for a finite positive measure $\lambda$, with projection $\mu$. Suppose also that $Q\in L^2$ is anti-invariant and that $P+Q$ is a closed current. Assume the estimates $$\begin{aligned}
\mu(B_r(x))&\le Cr^2,\label{eq:abstract-growth}\\
\iint_{d_g(x,y)<r}|\ell-\tau_{yx}m|^2
\,d\lambda(x,\ell)\,d\lambda(y,m)&\le Cr^4
\label{eq:abstract-angular}
\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently small $r$, uniformly in $x$. Then the limit $$\theta(x)=\lim_{r\downarrow0}r^{-2}\mu(B_r(x))$$ exists at every point. If $E=\{x:\theta(x)>0\}$, the current $P_E=\mathbf 1_EP$ is closed.*

</div>

For positive currents that are already closed, Elkhadhra proves that restriction to a $J$-analytic subset remains closed (Elkhadhra 2014, sec. 3.2, Lemma 1, author version) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Elkhadhra2014](#ref-Elkhadhra2014)]. Here only $P+Q$ is initially closed, and the positive-density set is not assumed to be $J$-analytic; the mass and angular estimates provide the cutoff control needed for the restriction.

Throughout this section and Section <a href="#sec:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:splitting">6</a>, assume the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:splitting">13</a>, and write $T=P+Q$. We continue to write $\mathcal I_r$ for the left side of <a href="#eq:abstract-angular" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:abstract-angular">[eq:abstract-angular]</a>. We first establish the density limit and show that, at $\mu$-almost every positive-density point, the current has planar tangent measures. This makes the average transverse displacement small without supplying a rate. Section <a href="#sec:splitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:splitting">6</a> combines that smallness with the angular bound to prove closedness.

<a id="stmt-lem-density"></a>
<div id="lem:density" class="lemma">

**Lemma 14**. *The two-density $\theta(p)$ exists and is finite at every $p\in X$.*

</div>

The radial comparison is in the tradition of Lelong–Jensen formulas for almost complex currents; see (Elkhadhra and Mimouni 2007, Proposition 1 and Corollary 2) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[ElkhadhraMimouni2007](#ref-ElkhadhraMimouni2007)]. We apply closure to $P+Q$ and estimate the $L^2$ correction directly.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-density"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-density</summary>

*Proof.* The current $T$ has measure coefficients with variation bounded by a constant times $\sigma=\mu+|Q|\,dV_g$. Cauchy–Schwarz and <a href="#eq:abstract-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:abstract-growth">[eq:abstract-growth]</a> give $$\sigma(B_s(p))\le Cs^2,$$ uniformly in $p$.

<a id="eq:density-identity"></a>
In normal coordinates $z$ at $p$, let $J_0=J(p)$ and $F_0=F_p$ be constant, and put $t=|z|=d_g(p,y)$. Our sign convention gives $dd^c_{J_0}(t^2/2)=2F_0$. Call a radius *good* if its sphere is $\sigma$-null. For good radii define $$b(r)=r^{-2}T(\mathbf 1_{B_r(p)}F_0).$$ These pairings are well-defined using the coefficient measures. We claim that $$\label{eq:density-identity}
b(s)-b(r)=\tfrac12
T\bigl(\mathbf 1_{\{r<t<s\}}dd^c_{J_0}\log t\bigr),
\qquad 0<r<s.$$

To verify it, define $$\phi_a(t)=
\begin{cases}
\log a+(t^2-a^2)/(2a^2),&t<a,\\
\log t,&t\ge a.
\end{cases}$$ The function $\phi_r-\phi_s$ is compactly supported and $C^{1,1}$. Its $dd^c_{J_0}$ is $2(r^{-2}-s^{-2})F_0$ on $B_r$, equals $dd^c_{J_0}\log t-2s^{-2}F_0$ on the annulus, and is zero outside $B_s$. For fixed $r,s$, compactly supported smooth approximations have uniformly bounded Hessians and converge in second derivatives off the two spheres. Dominated convergence against $\sigma$ permits testing closure on their exact two-forms and passing to the limit. The resulting identity is <a href="#eq:density-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:density-identity">[eq:density-identity]</a>.

<a id="eq:almost-monotone"></a>
<a id="eq:density-error"></a>
The form $dd^c_{J_0}\log t$ is $J_0$-invariant and semipositive, with size $O(t^{-2})$. Since $J(y)-J_0=O(t)$, it evaluates on actual complex line bivectors to at least $-C/t$, and its $J$-anti-invariant projection has size $O(1/t)$. The latter estimate controls its pairing with $Q$. Inward dyadic shells and the quadratic bound on $\sigma$ give $$\int_{\{0<t<s\}}t^{-1}\,d\sigma\le Cs.$$ It follows that $$\label{eq:almost-monotone}
